∎

5.3Proof of Theorem
4.1

At this point we are ready to prove the isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
.
Proving that we have
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}\subset\mathcal{F}
as a subrepresentation is straightforward to prove with an algebra-theoretic argument presented in the following lemma.
The opposite inclusion requires more work and is the essence of the proof of Theorem
4.1
below.

In the lemma, we use the following common notation:
given a vector
v
in a
\textnormal{{SF}}\oplus\overline{\textnormal{{SF}}}
representation
V
, we let
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})v
denote the subrepresentation of
V
cyclically generated by the action of
\textnormal{{SF}}\oplus\overline{\textnormal{{SF}}}
on
v
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
V
be a representation
4
4

4

We use the module notation for simplicity.
of
\textnormal{{SF}}\oplus\overline{\textnormal{{SF}}}
, and let
v\in V\setminus\{0\}
be a non-zero vector satisfying

\displaystyle\mspace{130mu}{\upchi}_{k}v\,=\,{\upeta}_{k}v\,=\,\mathrlap{\hskip-0.6pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\upchi$}}}}\upchi_{k}v\,=\,\mathrlap{\hskip-0.6pt\overline{\scalebox{0.97}[1]{\phantom{$\upeta$}}}}\upeta_{k}v\,=\,0

\displaystyle\phantom{\Big|}\mspace{50mu}\text{ for }\mspace{5mu}k>0\,,

\displaystyle\big({\upeta}_{0}-\mathrlap{\hskip-0.6pt\overline{\scalebox{0.97}[1]{\phantom{$\upeta$}}}}\upeta_{0}\big)v\,=\,0\,,\mspace{50mu}\big({\upchi}_{0}-\mathrlap{\hskip-0.6pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\upchi$}}}}\upchi_{0}\big)v\,

\displaystyle\,=\,0\,,\phantom{\Big|}

and

\displaystyle{\upchi}_{0}{\upeta}_{0}v\,\neq\,0\,.

Then there exists a unique
(\textnormal{{SF}}\oplus\overline{\textnormal{{SF}}})
-homomorphism
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}\rightarrow V
determined by

\displaystyle\bm{\omega}\longmapsto v\,.

This map is injective and constitutes an isomorphism between
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
and
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})v
as representations of
\textnormal{{SF}}\oplus\overline{\textnormal{{SF}}}
.

Proof.

The existence of such a map follows from the universal property of the quotient
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
.
Uniqueness follows from the fact that the values of the map on the basis vectors are fixed by
\bm{\omega}\mapsto v
and the homomorphism property.
Let
\phi\colon\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}\rightarrow V
denote such a unique homomorphism.
By construction, the map
\phi
maps
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
surjectively onto the submodule
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})v
.
As for injectivity, since it is a non-zero map, its kernel must be a proper submodule of
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
.
Recall from Remark
2.1
that the only three proper submodules of
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}

are
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\xi}
,
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\theta}
and
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\mathbbm{1}}
; the third one, in turn, is a proper submodule of each of the first two.
However, since we assumed

\displaystyle\phi(\bm{\mathbbm{1}})\,=\,\phi({\upchi}_{0}{\upeta}_{0}\bm{\omega})\,=\,{\upchi}_{0}{\upeta}_{0}\phi(\bm{\omega})\,=\,{\upchi}_{0}{\upeta}_{0}v\,\neq\,0\,,

we must have
\ker\phi=\{0\}
, i.e.
\phi
is injective.
We conclude that
\phi
is an isomorphism between
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
and
(\mathcal{SF}\otimes\mathrlap{\hskip 1.5pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})v
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

The local field
\bm{\upomega}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{\chi}_{k}\bm{\upomega}\,=\,\bm{\eta}_{k}\bm{\upomega}\,=\,\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{k}\bm{\upomega}\,=\,\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{k}\bm{\upomega}\,=\,0

for all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
and

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\chi}_{0}-\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{0}\big)\bm{\upomega}\,=\,0\,=\,\big(\bm{\eta}_{0}-\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{0}\big)\bm{\upomega}\,.

Moreover, we have
\bm{\chi}_{0}\bm{\eta}_{0}\bm{\upomega}=\mathbf{1}\neq 0
—Example
3.4
—.
Hence, by Lemma
5.12
, the subrepresentaion
(\mathcal{SF}\oplus\mathrlap{\hskip 2.5pt\overline{\scalebox{0.89}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\upomega}
generated by the cyclic action of the current modes on
\bm{\upomega}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
.
Since we have the inclusion
(\mathcal{SF}\oplus\mathrlap{\hskip 2.5pt\overline{\scalebox{0.89}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\upomega}\subset\mathcal{F}
, it only remains to prove
\mathcal{F}\subset(\mathcal{SF}\oplus\mathrlap{\hskip 2.5pt\overline{\scalebox{0.89}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\upomega}
.
Take any field
F\in\mathcal{F}
.
Since normal ordering is a surjective map —Remark
5.8
—, we can find linear field polynomials
L_{1},\ldots,L_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{\textnormal{lin}}
such that we have

\displaystyle F\,=\,\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\big(L_{1}+\textnormal{\small Null}\big)\cdots\big(L_{n}+\textnormal{\small Null}\big)\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\,.

Since
L_{i}
are linear, they must be a linear combination of the elements in the basis

\displaystyle\big\{\bm{\chi}_{-k}\mathbf{1},\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-k}\mathbf{1},\bm{\eta}_{-k}\mathbf{1},\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-k}\mathbf{1},\,\colon k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\big\}\,\cup\,\big\{\bm{\uptheta},\bm{\upxi}\big\}\,.

Finally, by Lemma
5.10
, the multilinearlity of normal ordering, and the relations between ground states via the current modes —Remark
3.13
—, the local field
F
can be written as an element of the submodule
(\mathcal{SF}\oplus\mathrlap{\hskip 2.5pt\overline{\scalebox{0.89}[1]{\phantom{$\mathcal{SF}$}}}}\mathcal{SF})\bm{\upomega}
.
∎

5.4Proof of Theorem
4.2

We now move on to the scaling limit of correlation functions.
In the proof of our second main theorem, we use the following self-contained results.
Consider the scaling-limit setting as in Theorem
4.2
.
For the rest of the section we define
\mathsf{C}\coloneqq\gamma+\frac{3}{2}\log 2
where
\gamma
is the Euler–Mascheroni constant.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\mathbf{z}^{\delta}\in\Omega^{\delta}
be the closest vertex to
z\in\Omega
in
\Omega^{\delta}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\mathbf{z}^{\delta},\mathbf{z}^{\delta})+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\delta\mspace{20mu}\xrightarrow{\mspace{40mu}\delta\downarrow 0\mspace{40mu}}\mspace{20mu}\mathbf{g}_{\Omega}(z,z)+\frac{\mathsf{C}}{2\pi}\,,

uniformly for
z
in compacts of
\Omega
, where
\mathbf{g}_{\Omega}
is the harmonic part of the Green’s function on the domain
\Omega
as in Section
2.1.2
.

Proof.

The function,

\displaystyle H^{\delta}\,\colon\,\mathbf{w}\longmapsto\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big(\mathbf{w},\mathbf{z}^{\delta}\big)-\mathbf{G}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{z}^{\delta}}{\delta}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\delta-\frac{\mathsf{C}}{2\pi}

is discrete harmonic on
\Omega^{\delta}
and has boundary values
\frac{1}{2\pi}\log|\mathbf{x}^{\delta}-\mathbf{z}^{\delta}|+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{2})
as
\delta\downarrow 0
for
\mathbf{x}^{\delta}
at the boundary of
\Omega^{\delta}
.
It is well-known that (any reasonable extension to
\Omega
of)
H^{\delta}
converges to the harmonic extension of the function
x\mapsto\frac{1}{2\pi}\log|x-z|
on
\partial\Omega
to the interior
\Omega
.
This limiting function on
\Omega
is the harmonic part of the Green’s function
\mathbf{g}_{\Omega}(\,\cdot\,,z)
.
Evaluating
H^{\delta}
at
\mathbf{z}^{\delta}
, we get the desired scaling limit convergence since we have
\mathbf{G}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}(0)=0
.
∎

Corollary 5.14
.

Let
\mathbf{z}^{\delta}\in\Omega^{\delta}
be the closest vertex to
z\in\Omega
in
\Omega^{\delta}
and fix
\lambda>0
.
We have

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{z}^{\delta})\theta(\mathbf{z}^{\delta})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}-2\,\log\big(\lambda\delta\big)\mspace{20mu}\xrightarrow{\mspace{40mu}\delta\downarrow 0\mspace{40mu}}\mspace{20mu}\Big\langle\bm{\omega}(z)\Big\rangle_{\Omega;\alpha(\lambda)}^{\textnormal{SyFe}}

uniformly for
z
in compacts of
\textnormal{Conf}_{n}(\Omega)
, where
\alpha(\lambda)\coloneqq 2\big(\log\lambda+\mathsf{C}\big)
.

Proof.

Straightforward from Lemma
5.13
and Example
2.5
.
∎

We are now ready to prove the main theorem.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

By linearity of the correlation functions, we only need to prove the statement for fields
\bm{\varphi}_{i}
in the basis (
4.1
).
For the sake of brevity, we detail the proof in the case where we have

\displaystyle\bm{\varphi}_{i}\,\coloneqq\,\bm{\chi}_{-k_{r_{i}}^{(i)}}\cdots

\displaystyle\bm{\chi}_{-k_{1}^{(i)}}\bm{\eta}_{-\ell_{s_{i}}^{(i)}}\cdots\bm{\eta}_{-\ell_{1}^{(i)}}\bm{\upomega}\in\mathcal{F}\,

with

{0\leq k_{1}^{(i)}<k_{2}^{(i)}<\cdots<k_{r_{i}}^{(i)}}

and

{0\leq\ell_{1}^{(i)}<\ell_{2}^{(i)}<\cdots<\ell_{s_{i}}^{(i)}}
—the more general case can be handled in a similar way.

The CFT correlation functions of the fields
\bm{\varphi}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\varphi}_{n}\in\mathscr{F}{\!\!\textnormal{\footnotesize ock}}
(
\cong\mathcal{F}
) can be expressed —recall Remark
2.4
— as

\displaystyle\Big\langle\bm{\varphi}_{1}(z_{1})\,\cdots\,\bm{\varphi}_{n}(z_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega;\alpha}^{\textnormal{SyFe}}\,=\phantom{\Bigg|}

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mspace{60mu}=\,\oint\!\cdots\!\oint\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\prod_{a=1}^{r_{i}}\frac{\textnormal{d}\zeta_{i;a}^{\eta}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\prod_{b=1}^{s_{i}}\frac{\textnormal{d}\zeta_{i;b}^{\chi}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\Bigg)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\prod_{a=1}^{r_{i}}\,\big(\zeta_{i;a}^{\eta}-z_{i}\big)^{-k_{a}^{(i)}}\prod_{b=1}^{s_{i}}\,\big(\zeta_{i;b}^{\chi}-z_{i}\big)^{-\ell_{b}^{(i)}}\Bigg)\times

\displaystyle\mspace{150mu}\phantom{\Bigg|}\times\bigg\langle\bm{\eta}\big(\zeta_{1;1}^{\eta}\big)\cdots\bm{\eta}\big(\zeta_{1;r_{1}}^{\eta}\big)\bm{\chi}\big(\zeta_{1;1}^{\chi}\big)\cdots\bm{\chi}\big(\zeta_{1;s_{1}}^{\chi}\big)\,\bm{\omega}(z_{1})\;\cdots

\displaystyle\mspace{290mu}\cdots\;\bm{\eta}\big(\zeta_{n;1}^{\eta}\big)\cdots\bm{\eta}\big(\zeta_{n;r_{n}}^{\eta}\big)\bm{\chi}\big(\zeta_{n;1}^{\chi}\big)\cdots\bm{\chi}\big(\zeta_{n;s_{n}}^{\chi}\big)\,\bm{\omega}(z_{n})\bigg\rangle_{\Omega;\alpha}^{\textnormal{SyFe}}\,,

where the variables
\zeta_{i;a}^{\eta}
and
\zeta_{i;b}^{\chi}
are integrated, respectively, along contours
\Gamma_{i;a}^{\eta}
and
\Gamma_{i;b}^{\chi}
in
\Omega
that encircle the point
z_{i}
and no other insertion point, and such that all contours are pairwise non-intersecting.
An example of this scenario is shown in Figure
5.1
.

In what remains of this proof, we omit some details—we refer the reader to the proof of Theorem 7.2 in
[
ABK24
]
.
In particular, in there, the contours and their discrete approximations are deliberately chosen to be of square shape so that some technical aspects substantially simplify.

To set up the scaling-limit convergence,
we let
\gamma_{i;a}^{\eta}(\delta)
and
\gamma_{i;b}^{\chi}(\delta)
be discrete corner contours
5
5

5

Naturally, one needs to impose certain requirements to these discrete contours so that in the limit
\delta\downarrow 0
one gets proper Riemann-sum approximations of continuum contour integrals.
in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
that are, respectively, close to the blown-up contours
\frac{1}{\delta}\big(\Gamma_{i;a}^{\eta}-z_{i}\big)
and
\frac{1}{\delta}\big(\Gamma_{i;b}^{\chi}-z_{i}\big)
.
Then, for the field
\bm{\varphi}_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
, consider the
\mathcal{P}
-representative

\displaystyle P_{i}^{\delta}\,\coloneqq\,\prod_{a=1}^{r_{i}}\Bigg(\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\eta}_{i;a}(\delta)}\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{u}_{i;a}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}(\mathbf{u}_{i;a})_{\diamond}^{[-k_{a}^{(i)}]}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big((\mathbf{u}_{i;a})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\Bigg)\times

\displaystyle\mspace{150mu}\times\,\prod_{b=1}^{s_{i}}\Bigg(\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\chi}_{i;b}(\delta)}\,\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{v}_{i;b}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}(\mathbf{v}_{i;b})_{\diamond}^{[-\ell_{b}^{(i)}]}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big((\mathbf{v}_{i;b})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\Bigg)\Big(-\bm{\upxi}(0)\bm{\uptheta}(0)-2\log\big(\lambda\delta\big)\Big)

of the field
\bm{\varphi}_{i}-\log(\lambda\delta)\big[({\mathbf{L}}_{0}-\mathrlap{\hskip-1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.85}[1]{\phantom{$\mathbf{L}$}}}}\mathbf{L}_{0})-(\Delta_{i}+\bar{\Delta}_{i})\big]\bm{\varphi}_{i}
,
where we have used the basis expression in Example
4.6
, the representative
\bm{\upomega}=-\bm{\upxi}(0)\bm{\uptheta}(0)+\textnormal{\small Null}
—Example
3.8
—, and the integral definition of the current modes —Proposition
3.9
—.

Figure 5.1:

On the left, a domain
\Omega
with insertion points and contours around

them.
One of the contours (
\Gamma_{i;1}
) around the point
z_{i}
is highlighted in orange.

On the right, the shifted and blown-up contour
\frac{1}{\delta}\big(\Gamma_{i;1}-z_{i}\big)
(orange) and

its closest discrete contour
\gamma_{i;1}(\delta)
.

The relevant fDGFF correlation functions are then

\displaystyle\Big\langle\textnormal{ev}_{\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\Omega^{\delta}}\big(P_{1}\big)\cdots\textnormal{ev}_{\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}}^{\Omega^{\delta}}\big(P_{n}\big)\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,=\phantom{\Bigg|}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mspace{20mu}=\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\prod_{a=1}^{r_{i}}\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\eta}_{i;a}(\delta)}\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{u}_{i;a}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\,(\mathbf{u}_{i;a})_{\diamond}^{[-k_{a}^{(i)}]}\prod_{b=1}^{s_{i}}\;\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\chi}_{i;b}(\delta)}\,\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{v}_{i;b}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}(\mathbf{v}_{i;b})_{\diamond}^{[-\ell_{b}^{(i)}]}\Bigg)\times

\displaystyle\mspace{30mu}\phantom{\Bigg|}\times\bigg\langle\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{u}_{1;1})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\cdots\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{u}_{1;r_{1}})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\,\cdot

\displaystyle\mspace{90mu}\cdot\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{v}_{1;1})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\cdots\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{v}_{1;s_{1}})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\Big(-\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}\big)\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\delta}\big)-2\log\big(\lambda\delta\big)\Big)\cdots

\displaystyle\mspace{90mu}\cdots\;\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{u}_{n;1})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\cdots\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{u}_{n;r_{n}})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\cdot\phantom{\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{90mu}\cdot\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{v}_{n;1})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\cdots\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{v}_{n;s_{n}})_{\textnormal{m}}\big)\Big(-\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}\big)\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{n}^{\delta}\big)-2\log\big(\lambda\delta\big)\Big)\bigg\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\!\!\!.

Let us focus on the integrand that is an fDGFF correlation function.
By the fDGFF generalised Wick’s formula —Remark
3.2
—, and by linearity of the fDGFF correlation functions, it can be rewritten as sums of products of factors of the two following types:

\displaystyle\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\eta}_{i;a}(\delta)}\!\!\!\!\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{u}_{i;a}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\,(\mathbf{u}_{i;a})_{\diamond}^{[-k_{a}^{(i)}]}\sqint^{\sharp}_{\gamma^{\chi}_{j;b}(\delta)}\,\!\!\!\!\frac{\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}\mathbf{v}_{j;b}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}(\mathbf{v}_{i;b})_{\diamond}^{[-\ell_{b}^{(j)}]}\big(\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\big)_{\!\mathbf{u}}\big(\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\big)_{\!\mathbf{v}}\Big\langle\theta(\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{u}_{i;a})_{\textnormal{m}})\xi(\mathbf{z}_{j}^{\delta}+\delta(\mathbf{v}_{j;b})_{\textnormal{m}})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}

and

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\xi\big(\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\delta}\big)\theta\big(\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\delta}\big)\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}-2\log\big(\lambda\delta\big)\,.

The latter type of factor, converges to

\displaystyle\Big\langle\bm{\omega}(x)\Big\rangle_{\Omega;\alpha(\lambda)}^{\textnormal{SyFe}}

uniformly for
x
on compacts of
\Omega
by Corollary
5.14
.
The former type, when renormalised by
\delta^{-(k_{a}^{(i)}+\ell_{b}^{(j)})}
, converges uniformly for
(z_{i},z_{j})
on compacts of
\textnormal{Conf}_{2}(\Omega)
to

\displaystyle\oint_{\Gamma^{\eta}_{i;a}}\!\!\frac{\textnormal{d}\zeta^{\eta}_{i;a}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\,\big(\zeta^{\eta}_{i;a}\big)^{-k_{a}^{(i)}}\sqint^{\sharp}_{\Gamma^{\chi}_{j;b}}\,\!\!\frac{\textnormal{d}\zeta^{\chi}_{j;b}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\,\big(\zeta^{\chi}_{j;b}\big)^{-\ell_{b}^{(j)}}\partial_{\zeta^{\eta}_{i;a}}\partial_{\zeta^{\chi}_{j;b}}\Big\langle\bm{\theta}\big(z_{i}+\zeta^{\eta}_{i;a}\big)\,\bm{\xi}\big(z_{j}+\zeta^{\chi}_{j;b}\big)\Big\rangle_{\Omega;\alpha(\lambda)}^{\textnormal{SyFe}}\,,

where we took
\alpha=\alpha(\lambda)
since the CFT correlation does not depend on this constant.
To see this, recall that for
\mathbf{u}^{\delta}\in\delta{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{\diamond}
satisfying
\delta\mathbf{u}^{\delta}\to\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
as
\delta\downarrow 0
, we have —Proposition
2.8
(Property 8)— the convergence of the discrete monomials

\displaystyle\delta^{k}\big(\mathbf{u}^{\delta}\big)^{[k]}\;\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\;\delta\downarrow 0\;\;\;}\;\zeta^{k}

uniformly for
\zeta
on compacts of
{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}
.
We also need the classical result of the scaling-limit convergence of the double derivatives of the discrete (Dirichlet) Green’s function to its continuum counterpart when renormalized by
\delta^{2}
—see
[
ABK24
, Section 3.3]
.
Then, when each of the discrete differentials
\textnormal{d}^{\sharp}
is renormalised by
\delta
, the expression
(
5.5
)
becomes a Riemann-sum approximation of double integrals.
Collecting the powers of
\delta
we find that, if we renormalise (
5.5
) by

\displaystyle{{{\delta}}^{\,-\sum_{i}\big(\sum_{a}k_{i;a}+\sum_{b}\ell_{i;b}\big)}},

it converges uniformly on compacts to (
5.4
) —after a shift in the integration variables—.
Note that
\sum_{a}k_{i;a}+\sum_{b}\ell_{i;b}
is the generalised scaling dimension of the field
\bm{\varphi}_{i}
.
∎

5.5Proof of Lemma
4.12

We now present the proof of the connection between the corelation functions of the fDGFF and those of the height-one field in presence of dissipations in the Abelian sandpile model.

Proof of Lemma
4.12
.

Via the burning algorithm, recurrent configuration with dissipative sites at
\mathbf{W}
are in bijection with spanning forests of
\Omega^{\delta}
where
\partial\Omega^{\delta}
and each of the dissipative sites
\mathbf{w}_{i}
belongs in a different tree.
Equivalently, these configurations are in bijection with spanning trees of the graph obtained from
(V_{\Omega^{\delta}},E_{\Omega^{\delta}})
by connecting each dissipative site
\mathbf{w}_{i}
to the boundary vertex
\partial\Omega^{\delta}
with a new edge—let
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
denote this modified graph.
Let
\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}
be the Laplacian matrix of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
with the row and column of the boundary site removed.

Let us check that the Green’s function of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}

—that is, up to a negative sign, the inverse matrix of
\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}
—
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\,=\,\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}{\big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}\,.

First, note that we have the decomposition of the modified Laplacian
\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}=\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}-\sum_{i}\mathbbm{1}^{\mathbf{w}_{i}}
with
(\mathbbm{1}^{\mathbf{w}})_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneqq\delta_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{w}}\delta_{\mathbf{y},\mathbf{w}}
.
From the fermionic Wick’s formula, we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{y})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\phantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{100mu}\phantom{\Bigg|}+\;\parbox{150.0pt}{\centering discrete harmonic terms \linebreak w.r.t.~$\mathbf{x}$ at $\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\mathbf{W}$.\@add@centering}

From this equality, it is clear that, for all
\mathbf{y}\in\Omega^{\delta}
and for all
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\mathbf{W}
, we indeed have
(\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}})_{(1)}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=-\delta_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}
, where the subscript
(1)
indicates the Laplacian is taken with respect to the first variable of
\tilde{\mathbf{G}}
.
Next, we use again Wick’s formula to write

\displaystyle\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,=\,\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{y})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\phantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{200mu}-\,\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{x})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{1})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{1})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{2})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\phantom{\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{200mu}+\,\parbox{150.0pt}{\centering discrete harmonic terms w.r.t.~$\mathbf{x}$ at $\mathbf{w}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{w}_{n}$.\@add@centering}

Hence, recalling the decomposition of
\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}})_{(1)}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})\,=\,

\displaystyle\,(\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}})_{(1)}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})-\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})\phantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,-\delta_{\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y}}\,+\,\frac{\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{1})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{2})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}{\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}-\,\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})\,.

However, since we have
\xi(\mathbf{w})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{1})=\xi(\mathbf{w})\theta(\mathbf{y})\big(\,1+\xi(\mathbf{w})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{1})\,\big)=\xi(\mathbf{w})\theta(\mathbf{y})
, it follows that we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})\,=\,\frac{\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{1})\theta(\mathbf{y})D(\mathbf{w}_{2})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}{\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{m})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}}\,,

which proves
(\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}})_{(1)}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y})=-\delta_{\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{y}}
for all
\mathbf{y}\in\Omega^{\delta}
.
Similar arguments prove that we also have
(\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}})_{(1)}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{w}_{i},\mathbf{y})=-\delta_{\mathbf{w}_{i},\mathbf{y}}
for
i=2,\ldots,n
, which proves
\tilde{\mathbf{G}}
to be the inverse of
\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}
(up to a negative sign).

At this point, similarly as in the case of the height-one field without the presence of dissipation fields, one can prove that we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\textnormal{ASM}}_{\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\mathbf{W}}\big[\,\mathsf{h}_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots\mathsf{h}_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{m})\big]\,=\,\Big\langle H_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{1})\cdots H_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{m})D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,,

where, recall, we defined
H_{1}(\mathbf{z})\coloneqq\frac{1}{4\pi}\big[\prod_{e\sim\mathbf{z}}\big(\nabla_{\!\sharp}\xi(e)\nabla_{\!\sharp}\theta(e)-1\big)-1\big]
.

To complete the proof, we just need to prove

\displaystyle\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,=\,\frac{\big|\mathcal{R}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big|}{\big|\mathcal{R}_{\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\mathbf{W}}\big|}\,.

Recall that recurrent configurations are in bijection with spanning trees via the burning algorithm.
Then, on the one hand, using the matrix-tree theorem, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\big|\mathcal{R}_{\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\mathbf{W}}\big|}{\big|\mathcal{R}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big|}\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\frac{|\det\tilde{\Delta}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}\,|}{|\det\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}\,|}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\frac{\big|\det\big(\,\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}-\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathbbm{1}^{\mathbf{w}_{i}}\big)\big|}{|\det\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}\,|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\frac{|\det\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}\,|\cdot\big|\det\big(\,\mathbb{I}+\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\mathbbm{1}^{\mathbf{w}_{i}}\big)\big|}{|\det\Delta_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\texttt{D}}\,|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\Big|\det\Big(\,\mathbb{I}+\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\mathbbm{1}^{\mathbf{w}_{i}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\big|\det\nolimits_{\mathbf{W}}(\,\mathbb{I}+\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}})\,\big|\phantom{\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}(-1)^{\sigma}\prod_{i=\sigma(i)}\Big(1+\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\mathbf{w}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)\prod_{j\neq\sigma(j)}\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\mathbf{w}_{j},\mathbf{w}_{\sigma(j)})

where
\det_{\mathbf{W}}
denotes the determinant of the submatrix with rows and columns indexed by
\mathbf{W}=\{\mathbf{w}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{w}_{n}\}
.
On the other hand, from the fermionic Wick’s formula, it is straightforward to get get

\displaystyle\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{1})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots\xi(\mathbf{w}_{n})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}=

\displaystyle\phantom{\bigg|}\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{1})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{1})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\!\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{2})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{2})\cdots\xi(\mathbf{w}_{n})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}

\displaystyle\mspace{40mu}\phantom{\Bigg|}+\,\sum_{\underset{\sigma(1)\neq 1}{\sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}}(-1)^{\sigma}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{i})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{\sigma(i)}\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\!\!\!,

from which one can compute

\displaystyle\Big\langle D(\mathbf{w}_{1})\cdots D(\mathbf{w}_{n})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}(-1)^{\sigma}\prod_{i=\sigma(i)}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{i})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\,\bigg)\,\times

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mspace{220mu}\phantom{\Bigg|}\times\prod_{j\neq\sigma(j)}\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big\langle\xi(\mathbf{w}_{j})\theta(\mathbf{w}_{\sigma(j)})\Big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}\!\!\!.

The complete the proof we just need to compare (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) since we have the
2
-point function
\big\langle\xi(\mathbf{z})\theta(\mathbf{w})\big\rangle_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{fDGFF}}=4\pi\,\mathbf{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})
.
∎

5.6Example of a basis computation

In this subsection we present a detailed computation for expressing an fDGFF local field in the basis (
4.1
).
We consider such a computation for the open-edge field of the UST—its associated fDGFF local field is

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\!\bullet\!\mathbf{-}}\,=\,\frac{1}{4\pi}\,\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,.

We consider first the linear local fields
\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}
and
\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}
separately.
We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\bm{\upxi}(1)-\bm{\upxi}(0)\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\phantom{\Big|}

(5.8)

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\bm{\upxi}(1)\,-\,\frac{1}{4}\Big[\bm{\upxi}(1)+\bm{\upxi}(\mathbbm{i})+\bm{\upxi}(-1)+\bm{\upxi}(-\mathbbm{i})\Big]\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\frac{\bm{\upxi}(1)-\bm{\upxi}(-1)}{2}\,+\,\frac{1}{4}\Big[\Big(\bm{\upxi}(1)+\bm{\upxi}(\mathbbm{i})\Big)-\Big(\bm{\upxi}(-1)+\bm{\upxi}(-\mathbbm{i})\Big)\Big]\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,

where we used the fact that
\Delta_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}(0)
is null.

A priori, a linear local field with only
\bm{\upxi}
-insertions, can be a linear combination of the basis fields
\bm{\upxi}
,
\bm{\chi}_{-k}\mathbf{1}
and
\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-k}\mathbf{1}
with
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
However, from
[
ABK24
]
we have the following insight:
a zero-average linear local fields with
\bm{\upxi}
-insertions in a ball of radius
1
can be expressed as a linear combination of the local fields
\bm{\chi}_{-1}\mathbf{1}
,
\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\mathbf{1}
and
\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}
.

From Example
5.2
, we can find the representatives

\displaystyle\bm{\chi}_{-1}\mathbf{1}\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{\textnormal{m}}}\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial_{\sharp}\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}}^{[-1]}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}(\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}})\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\upxi}(1)-\bm{\upxi}(-1)}{2}-\mathbbm{i}\,\frac{\bm{\upxi}(\mathbbm{i})-\bm{\upxi}(-\mathbbm{i})}{2}\bigg]\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\mathbf{1}\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{\textnormal{m}}}\partial_{\sharp}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\textnormal{m}}^{[-1]}\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}(\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}})\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\upxi}(1)-\bm{\upxi}(-1)}{2}+\mathbbm{i}\,\frac{\bm{\upxi}(\mathbbm{i})-\bm{\upxi}(-\mathbbm{i})}{2}\bigg]\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,,

and

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{\textnormal{m}}}\Big(\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial_{\sharp}\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}}^{[-2]}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}(\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}})+\partial_{\sharp}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\textnormal{m}}^{[-2]}\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\sharp}\bm{\upxi}(\mathbf{u}_{\textnormal{m}})\Big)\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\phantom{\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\;\frac{1}{2}\;\bigg[\Big(\bm{\upxi}(1)+\bm{\upxi}(-1)\Big)-\Big(\bm{\upxi}(\mathbbm{i})+\bm{\upxi}(-\mathbbm{i})\Big)\bigg]\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,,\phantom{\Bigg|}

where we have used the values of the discrete residues of the first two negative-power discrete monomials from Proposition
2.8
—these values are shown in Figure
5.2
.

Figure 5.2:

The values of the discrete residues of the first two negative-power discrete monomials.
On the left, the values of
\frac{1}{2\pi}\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial_{\sharp}\mathbf{u}^{[-1]}
;
on the right, the values of
\frac{1}{2\pi}\mathrlap{\hskip 1.0pt\overline{\scalebox{0.8}[1]{\phantom{$\partial$}}}}\partial_{\sharp}\mathbf{u}^{[-2]}
.

Using the expression in (
5.8
), it is clear that we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,=\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\big)\mathbf{1}+\frac{1}{2}\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}\,.

An identical computation yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,=\,\big(\bm{\eta}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-1}\big)\mathbf{1}+\frac{1}{2}\,\big(\bm{\eta}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}\,.

Now we just need to use Lemma
5.10
to write

\displaystyle\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\Big(\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}\Big)\otimes\Big(\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}\Big)\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\,=\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-1}\big)\mathbf{1}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mspace{190mu}\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Big[\big(\bm{\chi}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-2}\big)+\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-1}\big)\Big]\mathbf{1}\phantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{190mu}\,+\,\frac{1}{4}\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}\,.

On the other hand, by the definition of the normal ordering, we have

\displaystyle\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\Big(\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}\Big)\otimes\Big(\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)+\textnormal{\small Null}\Big)\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \circ\,\circ$}}\,\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \bullet\,\bullet$}}\,\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\,\rotatebox[]{90.0}{\scalebox{.8}{$\ \bullet\,\bullet$}}\,\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\phantom{\Big|}

(5.10)

\displaystyle\,=\,

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\phantom{\Big|}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\;\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\upxi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)\nabla_{\!\sharp}\bm{\uptheta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big)-\frac{4\pi}{2}\,+\,\textnormal{\small Null}\,,

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\;4\pi\,\mathsf{P}_{\!\bullet\!\mathbf{-}}

where we have used the values of the full-plane discrete Green’s function
\mathbf{G}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
to compute the Wick’s contractions.
Comparing (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), it becomes clear that we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\!\bullet\!\mathbf{-}}\,=\,\frac{1}{4\pi}\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-1}\big)\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle\mspace{120mu}\,+\,\frac{1}{8\pi}\,\Big[\big(\bm{\chi}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-1}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-2}\big)+\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-1}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-1}\big)\Big]\mathbf{1}\phantom{\bigg|}

\displaystyle\mspace{120mu}\,+\,\frac{1}{16\pi}\,\big(\bm{\chi}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\chi}$}}}}\bm{\chi}_{-2}\big)\big(\bm{\eta}_{-2}+\mathrlap{\hskip 0.0pt\overline{\scalebox{1}[1]{\phantom{$\bm{\eta}$}}}}\bm{\eta}_{-2}\big)\mathbf{1}\,.
